and
3.2
.

The next two Sections are devoted to the construction of a
generic set
of
error components initial data
satisfying these criteria.

5.3
Inversion of the perturbed Laplacian and smallness for the error term

This Section provides the basis for the construction of the
generic set
of admissible initial data (see Proposition
5.17
). In particular, it ensures that the
precise error initial data
(
5.5
) satisfy the constraint system (
5.3
). These results will be used crucially in the next Section. In addition, since we aim to construct solutions whose error term is small with respect to the parameter
\lambda
, we must impose suitable smallness assumptions on the corresponding initial data. Thus, in the Proposition below, we assume that the free component of the
error initial data

(z_{\lambda}^{i},\zeta_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i},\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda})
satisfy appropriate smallness conditions, and we determine the remaining component
(z_{\lambda}^{0},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{0})
through the equations. Moreover, an additional smallness assumption
\partial_{i}\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i}
is required to recover the smallness of
z^{0}_{\lambda}
. This is possible thanks to the freedom in the choice of initial data, although it makes the resulting precise error initial data slightly less generic.

During this entire Section, we denote with
C_{0}
any constant that only depends on the background data. See Notation
4.5
.

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
(z^{i}_{\lambda},\zeta_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i},\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda})\in H^{2}\times H^{2}\times H^{1}\times H^{1}
be compactly supported
13
13

13

We pick the same
S^{\prime}
as for the background in Proposition
4.1
without loss of generality.
in
B_{S^{\prime}}
and satisfy the following smallness conditions

\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\zeta_{\lambda}||_{H^{k+1}}+\lambda^{k}||\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda}||_{H^{k}}+\lambda^{k}||z^{i}_{\lambda}||_{H^{k+1}}+\lambda^{k}||\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i}||_{H^{k}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0},

(5.7)

together with

\displaystyle||\partial_{i}\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i}||_{L^{2}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0}.

(5.8)

Then, for any
-3/2<\delta<-1/2
there exists a solution
(z^{0}_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{0})\in H_{\delta}^{k+1}\times H_{\delta+1}^{k}
to (
5.3
) such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||z^{0}_{\lambda}||_{H_{\delta}^{k+1}}+\lambda^{k}||\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{0}||_{H_{\delta+1}^{k}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0},

(5.9)

where the weighted Sobolev norms are introduced in Definition
2.1
.

Proof.

For
\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{0}
, we use the first equation of (
5.3
). The desired estimate follows directly from the assumed smallness of the free data.

Remark 5.16
.

The polarization condition (
2.21
) is essential here, as it cancels potentially harmful terms of size
O(\lambda^{-1/2})
in the first constraint equation.

For
z^{0}_{\lambda}
, we use at the second equation of (
5.3
), involving the operator
-\Delta+|\phi_{1\lambda}+\zeta_{\lambda}|^{2}
.

If one were to invert this perturbed Laplacian directly
14
14

14

The perturbed Laplacian operator is only invertible in some weighted Sobolev spaces.
with Theorem
8.1
, one would generally obtain an estimate of the form
15
15

15

The constant in Lemma
8.14
only depends on
L^{2}
norms of
C_{0}
size initial data.

||z^{0}_{\lambda}||_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\sim\lambda^{-1/2}
due to the presence of several problematic source terms. This includes the derivative of the divergence of
W_{\mathscr{A}}
(see Remark
2.30
),
O(1)
high-frequency interactions and the term
\Im(\zeta_{\lambda}\overline{\dot{\phi}_{1\lambda}})
. The latter contains contributions of the form
-\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\Im(i\zeta_{\lambda}\lambda^{-1/2}\dot{v}_{\mathscr{A}}\overline{\psi}_{\mathscr{A}}e^{-i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}})
, which corresponds precisely to the obstruction described in
2)
of Section
3.2
. This difficulty is addressed at the level of the evolution equations by introducing the unknown
\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
.

At the level of the constraints, these obstructions are all treated by explicitly inverting by hand the
\lambda^{-1/2}
and
\lambda^{0}
terms.
We set

\displaystyle z^{0BIS}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{3/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}[\frac{\dot{v}_{\mathscr{A}}\Re\left(ie^{i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(\overline{\partial_{\alpha}W_{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha}})|_{t=0}\right)}{\partial^{i}v_{\mathscr{A}}\partial_{i}v_{\mathscr{A}}}]-\lambda^{3/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}[\frac{\Im\left(i\zeta_{\lambda}\dot{v}_{\mathscr{A}}\overline{\psi}_{\mathscr{A}}e^{-i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\right)}{\partial^{i}v_{\mathscr{A}}\partial_{i}v_{\mathscr{A}}}],

\displaystyle+\lambda^{2}\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\frac{\Re\left(\overline{K^{\beta}_{A(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})}e^{i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}\pm v_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\right)}{\partial^{i}(v_{\mathscr{A}}\pm v_{\mathscr{B}})\partial_{i}(v_{\mathscr{A}}\pm v_{\mathscr{B}})}+\lambda^{2}\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}\frac{\Re\left(\overline{S^{\beta}_{A(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\right)}{\partial^{i}(v_{\mathscr{A}}\pm v_{\mathscr{B}})\partial_{i}(v_{\mathscr{A}}\pm v_{\mathscr{B}})},

where the
K^{\beta}_{A(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})
’s and
S^{\beta}_{A(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})
’s terms are defined in Proposition
4.8
(here with respect to the background initial data) and arise from the term
\tilde{\xi}^{0}_{A\lambda}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})
of the second equation of (
5.3
). In particular,
K^{\beta}_{A(\mathscr{A}+\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})=S^{\beta}_{A(\mathscr{A}+\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})=0
.
By construction,
z^{0BIS}_{\lambda}
possesses the sufficient smallness and is compactly supported. More precisely, using Proposition
2.20
and estimates (
2.12
), (
2.13
), (
2.14
) to control the denominators, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||z^{0BIS}_{\lambda}||_{H^{k+1}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0}.

(5.10)

Then, we search for
z^{0TER}_{\lambda}
as a solution to

\begin{split}(-\Delta+|\phi_{1\lambda}+\zeta_{\lambda}|^{2})z_{\lambda}^{0TER}-\partial_{i}\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i}&=(\Delta-|\phi_{1\lambda}+\zeta_{\lambda}|^{2})z_{\lambda}^{0BIS}+\lambda^{1/2}\xi_{A\lambda}^{0}(\textbf{B}_{0})+\tilde{\xi}_{A\lambda}^{0}(\textbf{B}_{0})\\
&-\Im(\phi_{1\lambda}\overline{\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda}})-\Im(\zeta_{\lambda}\overline{\dot{\phi}_{1\lambda}})-\Im(\zeta_{\lambda}\overline{\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda}})-|\zeta_{\lambda}|^{2}a_{1\lambda}^{0}-(\zeta_{\lambda}\overline{\phi_{1\lambda}}+\overline{\zeta_{\lambda}}\phi_{1\lambda})a_{1\lambda}^{0}\\
&+\lambda^{1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\left(\partial_{t}\Re\left(e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(\overline{\partial_{\alpha}W_{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha}})\right)\right)|_{t=0}.\\
\end{split}

(5.11)

The RHS of (
5.11
) is now of order
O(\lambda^{1/2})
and is well defined in terms of regularity. In particular, the Laplacian of
\zeta_{\lambda}
appears and belongs to
L^{2}
because
\zeta_{\lambda}\in H^{2}
. The remaining terms only involve background terms which are more regular. Moreover, the quantity
|\phi_{1\lambda}+\zeta_{\lambda}|^{2}
belongs to
H^{2}
and is compactly supported. This allows us to use the Lemma
8.14
(as
-3/2<\delta<-1/2
) to get

\displaystyle||z_{\lambda}^{0TER}||_{H^{2}_{\delta}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0}.

In particular, the term
\Im(\zeta_{\lambda}\overline{\dot{\zeta}\lambda})
, which is problematic in the evolution equations (see item
4)
in Section
3.2
), is harmless at the level of the constraints due to the decoupled nature of the constraints. Indeed,

\displaystyle||\Im(\zeta_{\lambda}\overline{\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda}})||_{L^{2}}\leq||\zeta_{\lambda}||_{H^{1}}||\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda}||_{H^{1/2}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0},

where we used the product estimate (
8.29
) and the interpolation inequality (
8.20
). All other terms are easily controlled in
L^{2}
, using in particular the additional smallness (
5.8
) for
\partial_{i}\dot{z}^{i}_{\lambda}
. Finally, defining
z^{0}_{\lambda}=z^{0BIS}_{\lambda}+z^{0TER}_{\lambda}
, we obain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||z^{0}_{\lambda}||_{H_{\delta}^{k+1}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0},

which ends the proof.
∎

5.4
Construction of the precise error initial data

In the previous Section, we constructed small
error initial data

(z_{\lambda},\zeta_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda},\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda})
that solve the constraints (
5.3
). However, prescribing these initial data directly as initial conditions for the error evolution system (see (
6.5
)) does
not
provide the appropriate structure to close the bootstrap. Therefore, the purpose of this Section is to construct a generic set of
initial data
for the
error components
that are
admissible
in the sense of Remark
5.14
and that are based on the error initial data obtained in Proposition
5.15
.

Proposition 5.17
.

For a given admissible background initial data set
(a^{\alpha}_{0},\dot{a}^{\alpha}_{0},\phi_{0},\dot{\phi}_{0},v_{\mathscr{A}},\dot{v}_{\mathscr{A}},\psi_{\mathscr{A}},w^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}})
and for
\lambda>0
, Let
(z_{\lambda}^{0},z_{\lambda}^{i},\zeta_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{0},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i},\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda})
be given by Proposition
5.15
and
(e^{ell}_{\lambda},\epsilon^{ell}_{\lambda},\dot{e}^{ell}_{\lambda},\dot{\epsilon}^{ell}_{\lambda})
by Definition
5.8
. Then, for

\displaystyle(e^{evo}_{\lambda})^{\alpha}=z_{\lambda}^{\alpha}-(e^{ell}_{\lambda})^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\epsilon^{evo}_{\lambda}=\zeta_{\lambda}-\epsilon^{ell}_{\lambda},

\displaystyle\forall\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A},\;w^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=0,

\displaystyle\forall(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C},\;\breve{w}^{+\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}=0,

\displaystyle\forall\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A},\;\psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=0,

\displaystyle\forall(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C},\;\breve{\psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}=0,

\displaystyle\forall\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A},\;g^{+\alpha}_{W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}=(\Box W^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0},

\displaystyle\forall\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A},\;g^{+}_{\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}=(\Box\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0},

\displaystyle(\dot{e}^{evo}_{\lambda})^{\alpha}=\dot{z}^{\alpha}_{\lambda}-\lambda\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\Re\left(e^{\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(\overline{\partial_{t}W_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+\alpha}})|_{t=0}\right)-\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\lambda\Re\left(e^{\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}\pm v_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}(\overline{\partial_{t}\breve{W}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+\alpha}})|_{t=0}\right)-(\dot{e}^{ell}_{\lambda})^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\dot{\epsilon}^{evo}_{\lambda}=\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda}-\lambda\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}(\partial_{t}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}e^{i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}-\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\lambda\partial_{t}(\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})|_{t=0}e^{i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}\pm v_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}-\dot{\epsilon}^{ell}_{\lambda},

the initial data for the
error components
are
admissible for KGML
,
admissible for KGM in Lorenz gauge
and satisfy the
required smallness
.

Remark 5.18
.

This initial data set is generic in the sense that no additional freedom is used on
(z_{\lambda}^{0},z_{\lambda}^{i},\zeta_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{0},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i},\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda})
, aside from the convenient smallness and regularity conditions, together with the technical smallness of
\partial_{i}\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i}
. As already observed from estimates (
5.7
) and
(
5.9
), the error initial data
(z_{\lambda}^{0},z_{\lambda}^{i},\zeta_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{0},\dot{z}_{\lambda}^{i},\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda})
enjoy better
smallness properties than the full error terms
(Z_{\lambda},\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda})
(see point
2
of Theorem
3.2
) in terms of smallness. This improvement is due to the choice
(w^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\breve{w}^{+\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\breve{\psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})=0
, which yields additional smallness at time
t=0
and ensures sufficient regularity to
(g^{+\alpha}_{W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}},g^{+}_{\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}})
. At a later time, the extra smallness of the error initial data does not propagate, as there is no reason that
(W^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\breve{W}^{+\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})=0
, whereas the regularity of
(g^{+\alpha}_{W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}},g^{+}_{\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}})
does.

Overall, the freedom of choice is used only on the auxiliary components, while the
10
real initial data are only
subject to
2
constraints, retaining their
8
real degrees of freedom.

Proof of Proposition
5.17
.

For the KGML admissibility, it suffices to verify regularity and smallness. The estimates for
(z_{\lambda},\zeta_{\lambda},\dot{z}_{\lambda},\dot{\zeta}_{\lambda})

and
(e^{ell}_{\lambda},\epsilon^{ell}_{\lambda},\dot{e}^{ell}_{\lambda},\dot{\epsilon}^{ell}_{\lambda})
follow directly from Proposition
5.15
together with (
5.7
), (
5.9
), and (
5.6
). The only delicate part concerns the time derivatives of
W^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\breve{W}^{+\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
and
\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
. In particular, for
g^{+\alpha}_{W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
and
g^{+}_{\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
, Remark
5.11
and the fact that
w^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=0
and
\psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=0
by choice imply that, although
\Box
terms appear to involve two derivatives, it is not the case. Indeed, the two spatial derivatives vanish, and the two time derivatives only depend on quantities that involve one derivative with sufficient regularity. We have

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}W^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}=\lambda^{-1/2}f_{w}^{\alpha}(\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0})\epsilon^{evo}_{\lambda},

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}=\lambda^{-1/2}f_{\psi\alpha}(\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0})(e^{evo}_{\lambda})^{\alpha},

\displaystyle(\partial^{2}_{tt}W^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}=L^{\alpha}_{w}(\nabla(\partial_{t}W^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0},\lambda^{-1/2}\dot{\epsilon}^{evo}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0})+Q^{\alpha}_{w}(\epsilon^{evo}_{\lambda},e^{evo}_{\lambda})+[\ldots],

\displaystyle(\partial^{2}_{tt}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}=L^{\alpha}_{\psi}(\nabla(\partial_{t}\Phi^{+\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0},\lambda^{-1/2}\dot{e}^{evo}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0})+Q^{\alpha}_{\psi}(e^{evo}_{\lambda},e^{evo}_{\lambda})+[\ldots],

where the
[\ldots]
involve lower-order more regular terms. The
f_{w}
and
f_{\psi}
terms involve no derivatives of
\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0}
,
L_{w}
and
L_{\psi}
depends linearly on their first two parameters, and
Q_{w}
and
Q_{\psi}
contain a product with respect to their two parameters. All these contributions are controlled using (
2.11
) and the estimates of Proposition
5.15
and Definition
2.25
. To estimate
\dot{e}^{evo}_{\lambda}
and
\dot{\epsilon}^{evo}_{\lambda}
, we use

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\lambda e^{i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(\partial_{t}W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}||_{H^{k}}\leq\lambda C_{0},

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\lambda e^{i\frac{v_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(\partial_{t}\Phi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}||_{H^{k}}\leq\lambda C_{0},

where
C_{0}
is as in Notation
4.5
, other direct estimates. For
g^{+\alpha}_{W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
and
g^{+}_{\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
, we use
C_{0}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||(\partial^{2}_{tt}W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}||_{H^{k}}\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||(\partial^{2}_{tt}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})|_{t=0}||_{H^{k}}\leq C_{0},

so that we have the required smallness and the required regularity of Definition
5.13
. In particular, the quantities
g^{+\alpha}_{W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
and
g^{+}_{\Phi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
are more regular than two standard derivatives of
W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
and
\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
are expected to be.

Finally, the KGM in Lorenz gauge admissibility follows because our
precise error initial data
are constructed on the
error initial data
from Definition
5.5
, which directly implies that
(a^{\alpha}_{\lambda},\dot{a}^{\alpha}_{\lambda},\phi_{\lambda},\dot{\phi}_{\lambda})
is solution to the constraint equations (
5.2
).
∎

6
Exact multiphase high-frequency solutions

6.1
Generalities on the error term and evolution equations

Recall that we seek exact solutions
(A_{\lambda},\Phi_{\lambda})_{0<\lambda}
to KGM under the form:

A^{\alpha}_{\lambda}=A^{\alpha}_{1\lambda}+Z_{\lambda}^{\alpha},\;\;\;\;\Phi_{\lambda}=\Phi_{1\lambda}+\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda},

(6.1)

which satisfy the Lorenz gauge condition
\partial_{\alpha}A^{\alpha}_{\lambda}=0
. In this Section, we assume that

\displaystyle A^{\alpha}_{1\lambda}=A^{\alpha}_{0}+\lambda^{1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}\right)P^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}+\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}\right)Q^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}},

\displaystyle\Phi_{1\lambda}=\Phi_{0}+\lambda^{1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}},

(6.2)

are
first-order almost approximate solutions
to KGM in Lorenz gauge, in the sense of Definition
2.28
. These solutions are constructed via Proposition
4.1
, and more generally Section
4
, and are based on an admissible initial ansatz, in the sense of Definition
2.25
. In other words, we assume that step
(i)
of Section
3.2
is completed. We further assume that step
(ii)
is completed, namely that the initial data for (
6.1
) solve the constraint of KGM in Lorenz gauge (
5.2
), and that the initial data for the error term possesses both the appropriate structure and smallness properties. The construction of such initial data is detailed in Section
5
, and in particular in Proposition
5.17
. The present Section corresponds to steps
(iii)
,
(iv)
and
(v)
.

Notation 6.1
.

Since

\cos\left(\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}\right)P^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}+\sin\left(\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}\right)Q^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}
may be shorten as
\Re\left(e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\overline{W^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}}\right)
for
W^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}=P^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}+iQ^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}
, we introduce the schematic notation

\textbf{F}_{\lambda}=\underbrace{\textbf{F}_{0}+\lambda^{1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\textbf{F}_{\mathscr{A}}}_{\text{$\textbf{F}_{1\lambda}$}}+\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}.

(6.3)

Throughout the paper, bold symbols, such as
\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}
, denote both the potential and the wave function unknowns at a schematic level. Recall also Notation
4.6
,:

\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
denotes the background terms
(\textbf{F}_{0},\textbf{F}_{\mathscr{A}},\textbf{d}u_{\mathscr{A}})
, while
\textbf{B}_{0}
denotes the background terms excluding the phases
(\textbf{d}u_{\mathscr{A}})_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}
.

With Notation
6.1
, the KGM system (
1.1
) in Lorenz gauge, namely the KGML system (
1.12
), reduces to the schematic equation

\Box\textbf{F}_{\lambda}=\textbf{F}_{\lambda}\partial\textbf{F}_{\lambda}+(\textbf{F}_{\lambda})^{3}.

(6.4)

The gauge propagation is addressed in Section
6.6
.
The error term
\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}
(denoting
(Z_{\lambda},\mathcal{Z}_{\lambda})
) must therefore satisfy schematically

\Box\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}=\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}+\mathscr{L}_{1}\left(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\partial\textbf{B}_{0},\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0},\frac{\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{A}}}{\lambda}\right)+\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{A}}}{\lambda}\right)+\mathscr{H}\left(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{A}}}{\lambda}\right)+\lambda^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\lambda}(\textbf{B}_{0})+\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\lambda}(\textbf{B}_{0}),

(6.5)

so that the parametrix (
6.3
) solves (
6.4
), provided that
\textbf{F}_{1\lambda}
is an almost approximate solution to (
6.4
).
Here,
\mathscr{L}_{1}
(resp.
\mathscr{L}_{2}
) is linear in its first three (resp. first two) arguments and periodic in its fourth (resp. third) argument, while
\mathscr{H}
is at least quadratic and at most cubic in its first argument and periodic in its third argument. The nonlinear term
\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}
is explicit. The terms
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\lambda}(\textbf{B}_{0})
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\lambda}(\textbf{B}_{0})
denote the remainders arising from the approximation procedure introduced in Definition
2.28
, see Notation
4.7
.

The system (
6.5
) (represented here by a single equation) is clearly locally well-posed for
\lambda>0
and for initial data
(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\dot{\textbf{Z}}_{\lambda})\in H^{2}\times H^{1}
, although it is far from obvious that the time of existence is uniform in
\lambda
.
To obtain such a uniform time of existence, we refine the structure of the error term by writing it as

\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}=\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\lambda^{1}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}+\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\lambda^{1}\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}+\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}+\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}.

(6.6)

The components
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}
are the
decoupled error components
and solve

\forall(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C},\;\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}=\textbf{K}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})+(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial u_{\mathscr{B}})\textbf{B}_{0}\breve{\textbf{F}}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+},

(6.7)

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}=-\lambda^{2}\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{B}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\frac{\textbf{S}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})}{\partial_{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})\partial^{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})},

(6.8)

for
\textbf{S}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})
and
\textbf{K}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})
given by Proposition
4.8
and Notations
4.9
.

The components
(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo},\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})
, together with the auxiliary function
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
(introduced to handle the derivative loss discussed in point
3)
of Section
3.2
) are the
coupled error components
. They solve the coupled system of

\begin{cases}&\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}+\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(i\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})-\lambda^{1}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}},\\
\\
&\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0},\\
\\
&\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=[\mathscr{L}_{A},\Box]\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}+\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}+\Box(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}))\\
&+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})-\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\Box\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\textbf{B}_{0}+[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}},\\
\end{cases}

(6.9)

where the
[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}}
and the
[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
depend on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}
,
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
, the background
\textbf{B}_{0}
or lower-order terms. We refer to Sections
6.3.4
,
6.3.5
and
6.3.6
for their precise form. The projectors
\Pi_{\kappa,+}
and
\Pi_{\kappa,-}
are introduced in Definition
2.5
and depend on a parameter
\kappa>0
, which is fixed in Section
6.3.5
.

The decoupled error components obey linear transport equations or depend only on the background, and are therefore well-defined on the entire interval of existence
[0,T]
of the background solution. Consequently, the decoupled components are not part of the bootstrap argument. By contrast, the coupled error components satisfy nonlinear wave and transport equations that depend on both the background and the decoupled components, and therefore require a bootstrap argument to extend their existence interval up to
[0,T]
.

At the level of the error term, Steps
(iii)
,
(iv)
, and
(v)
are summarized by the following three propositions.

Proposition 6.2

(Exact solutions to KGML)
.

Let
(\textbf{f}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{g}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\breve{\textbf{f}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\textbf{e}^{evo}_{\lambda},\dot{\textbf{e}}^{evo}_{\lambda},\textbf{e}_{\lambda}^{ell},\dot{\textbf{e}}_{\lambda}^{ell})
be initial data for the error components that are
admissible for KGML
in the sense of Definition
5.10
. Then, there exist error components
(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{evo}_{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{ell}_{\lambda})
satisfying (
6.7
), (
6.8
) and (
6.9
). The associated error term
\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}
defined by (
6.6
), when added to the approximate solution
\textbf{F}_{1\lambda}
, yields an exact solution
\textbf{F}_{\lambda}=\textbf{F}_{1\lambda}+\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}
to KGML (
6.4
), defined on some interval
[0,t_{\lambda}]
.

Remark 6.3
.

In a non-schematic form, the parametrix
\textbf{F}_{\lambda}
reads

\begin{split}&A^{\alpha}_{\lambda}=A^{\alpha}_{0}+\lambda^{1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\Re\left(e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\overline{W^{\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}}}\right)+\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\lambda^{1}\Re\left(e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\overline{W^{+\alpha}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}\right)+\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\lambda^{1}\Re\left(e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\overline{\breve{W}^{+\alpha}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}}\right)\\
&+(E_{\lambda}^{evo})^{\alpha}+(E_{\lambda}^{ell})^{\alpha},\end{split}

(6.10)

\Phi_{\lambda}=\Phi_{0}+\lambda^{1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}+\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}\lambda^{1}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}+\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\lambda^{1}\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}+\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}^{evo}+\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}^{ell}.

(6.11)

Proposition 6.4

(Uniform existence time in
\lambda
)
.

Let
(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell},\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})
be given by the Proposition
6.2
. If, in addition, the initial data satisfy
the required smallness
in the sense of Definition
5.13
, then there exists
\lambda_{0}>0
, such that the family of solutions
(\textbf{F}_{\lambda})_{0<\lambda<\lambda_{0}}
is defined on the full interval
[0,T]
.

Proposition 6.5

(Exact solutions to KGM in Lorenz gauge)
.

Let
(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell},\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})
be given by the Proposition
6.2
. If the initial data are also
admissible for KGM in Lorenz gauge
in the sense of Definition
5.12
, then the parametrix
\textbf{F}_{\lambda}=\textbf{F}_{1\lambda}+\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}
is solution to KGM (
1.1
) in Lorenz gauge, i.e., the gauge propagates.

6.2
Decoupled error components

6.2.1
Information on
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}

Definition 6.6
.

For a given admissible background initial data, let
\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0}
be the associated background provided by Proposition
4.1
. We define the transport equation for
(\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}
by

\forall(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C},\;\;\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}=\textbf{K}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})+(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial u_{\mathscr{B}})\textbf{B}_{0}\breve{\textbf{F}}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+}.

(6.12)

Remark 6.7
.

The error components
(\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}
schematically represent
(\breve{W}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}
, see the parametrices (
6.10
). The transport equation (
6.12
) therefore corresponds, for all
(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}
, to

\begin{cases}\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}\breve{W}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+\beta}=i(\partial^{\beta}u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial^{\beta}u_{\mathscr{B}})\overline{\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}}\Phi_{0}+iK^{\beta}_{A(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}),\\
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}=-i2A^{\alpha}_{0}(\partial^{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial_{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{B}})\breve{\Psi}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}-iK_{\Phi(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}),\\
\end{cases}

(6.13)

where, in particular, the RHS of the equation for
\breve{W}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+\beta}
is orthogonal to
(\partial_{\beta}u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial_{\beta}u_{\mathscr{B}})
, see Proposition
4.11
.

Remark 6.8
.

The components
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
are introduced to absorb the term
\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{B}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\textbf{K}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(B_{0}^{\prime})
, i.e., the
O(1)
high-frequency terms oscillating along characteristic phases emerging from resonant interaction contained in
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\lambda}
. The equation (
6.12
) is
decoupled
from the system (
6.9
). For simplicity, and exploiting the freedom in the choice of these parameters, we impose
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}|_{t=0}=0
in Proposition
5.17
. Further details concerning the initial data are given in Section
5
.

Remark 6.9
.

The RHS term

\displaystyle(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial u_{\mathscr{B}})\textbf{B}_{0}\breve{\textbf{F}}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+}

(6.14)

originates from
\mathscr{L}_{2}(\partial\textbf{Z},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{A}}}{\lambda})
in (
6.5
), while
the term

\displaystyle\textbf{K}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})

(6.15)

arises from the decomposition of
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\lambda}
given in Proposition
4.8
.

The next proposition establishes the smallness and regularity properties of
(\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}
.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}\in H^{3}
with
Supp(\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})\subset\Omega
for
\Omega
a compact set of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
There exists a unique local solution
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}\in\cap^{3}_{j=0}(C^{j}([0,T],H^{3-j})
defined on the time interval
[0,T]
of existence of the background, and satisfying
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}|_{t=0}=\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
. Moreover,
Supp(\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}})\subset\mathscr{I}(T,\Omega)
and, for
C_{0}
from Notation
4.5
, the following estimate holds

\max_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\sum_{k\leq 3}(||\partial^{k}_{t}\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,T],H^{3-k})})\leq C_{0}.\\

(6.16)

Proof.

Existence and uniqueness follow from the standard energy method. Finite speed of propagation yields the support property. For (
6.16
), we refer to Section
8.1
. For (
6.16
), we refer to Section
8.1
. In particular, we apply Proposition
16
16

16

The linear term on the RHS of (
8.10
) is harmless and can be controlled in the same way as the other linear term appearing in the evolution equation.

8.4
to obtain control of the
H^{3}
norm, using that
\breve{\textbf{f}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}\in H^{3}
. Then, we use Proposition
8.5
to control the time derivatives.

∎

Remark 6.11
.

We note that
\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
belongs to
H^{3}
for
t\in[0,T]
, just as the background does, and is more regular than the coupled components
\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
. This additional regularity is useful since the RHS of (
