)
contains the term
\Box\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
.

6.2.2
Information on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}

Definition 6.12
.

For a given admissible background initial data, let
\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0}
denote the background provided by Proposition
4.1
. We set

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}=-\lambda^{2}\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\frac{\textbf{S}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})}{\partial_{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})\partial^{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})}.

(6.17)

Remark 6.13
.

Non-schematically, (
6.17
) reads

(E^{ell}_{\lambda})^{\alpha}=-\lambda^{2}\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}\frac{\Re\left(\overline{S^{\alpha}_{A(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\right)}{\partial_{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})\partial^{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})},\\

(6.18)

\mathcal{E}^{ell}_{\lambda}=-\lambda^{2}\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}\frac{S_{\Phi(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}}{\partial_{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})\partial^{\alpha}(u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}})}.

(6.19)

We also refer to the non-schematic parametrices (
6.10
). These expressions are well defined since the denominator is uniformly bounded away from zero, see Proposition
2.20
.

Remark 6.14
.

The component
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}
is introduced to absorb the
O(1)
high-frequency terms oscillating along non-characteristic phases emerging from non-resonant interactions contained in
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{\lambda}
. More precisely,

\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}-\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\textbf{S}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})=O(\lambda^{1})f(\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0}),

(6.20)

while, at the same time,
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}
remains sufficiently small as an error component, as quantified in the following proposition.

Proposition 6.15
.

We have
Supp(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell})\subset B_{S}
and, for
C_{0}
from Notation
4.5
, the estimates

\sum_{k\leq 2}\lambda^{k}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}||_{L^{\infty}([0,T],H^{k})}+\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k+1}||\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}||_{L^{\infty}([0,T],H^{k})}+\lambda^{2}||\partial^{2}_{tt}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}||_{L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2})}\leq\lambda^{2}C_{0},\\

(6.21)

and

\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}-\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\textbf{S}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}))||_{L^{\infty}([0,T],H^{k})}\leq\lambda^{1}C_{0}.\\

(6.22)

Proof.

Using the Definition
6.12
together with Remark
6.14
, the compact support property follows immediately. The estimates are obtained by combining (
2.15
), which provides uniform control on the smallness of the denominator, and point
1
of Proposition
4.1
, which provides bounds on the background
\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
.
∎

6.3
Coupled error components

6.3.1
System of evolution

The coupled component
(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo},\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})
, together with the auxiliary unknown
(\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})
, solves the system

\begin{cases}&\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}+\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(i\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})-\lambda^{1}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}},\\
\\
&\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0},\\
\\
&\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=[\mathscr{L}_{A},\Box]\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}+\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}+\Box(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})),\\
&+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})-\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\Box\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\textbf{B}_{0}+[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}.\\
\end{cases}

(6.23)

All terms appearing in (
6.23
) are detailed in the following Sections. In Section (
6.3.2
), we first establish the local well-posedness of (
6.23
). Then, in Section (
6.3.3
), we state suitable a priori bounds for the coupled error components. Through Sections
6.3.4
,
6.3.4
and
6.3.6
, we derive energy estimates for
\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
,
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
, respectively. Finally, in Sections
6.3.7
, we exhibit a certain
0<\lambda_{0}
such that the latter energy estimates allow one to improve the a priori bounds for
0<\lambda<\lambda_{0}
, thereby closing a bootstrap argument.

Notation 6.16
.

We denote by
\underline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{evo}_{\lambda}}
all the components of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
except
(E_{\lambda}^{evo})^{0}
in the parametrices introduced in Remark
6.3
. The quantity
(E_{\lambda}^{evo})^{0}
corresponds to the part of the error term associated with
A^{0}_{\lambda}
. Its initial data are obtained by solving the constraint equation (
5.2
). To invert the Laplacian, we resort to weighted Sobolev norms, which implies that
(E_{\lambda}^{evo})^{0}
is in
H^{2}_{\delta}
, for some
-3/2<\delta<-1/2
, and therefore need not be in
L^{2}
.

6.3.2
Well-posedness

In this Section, we establish the well-posedness of (
6.23
).

Proposition 6.17
.

For a given admissible background initial data set in the sense of Definition
2.25
, let
\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0}
be the background given by Proposition
4.1
, let
\lambda>0
and let
\kappa>0
be the constant appearing in the Definition
2.5
of the projectors
\Pi_{\kappa,-}
and
\Pi_{\kappa,+}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\breve{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
be given by Proposition
6.10
and let
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}
be given by Definition
6.12
from initial data
(\textbf{f}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{g}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\breve{\textbf{f}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\textbf{e}^{evo},\dot{\textbf{e}}^{evo},\textbf{e}^{ell},\dot{\textbf{e}}^{ell})
which are
admissible for KGML
in the sense of Definition
5.10
. Then, the Cauchy problem associated with (
6.23
) is locally well-posed for the initial data
(\textbf{f}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{g}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\textbf{e}^{evo},\dot{\textbf{e}}^{evo})
. There exists a time
t_{\lambda}
such that there exists a solution
(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo})
with the following regularity

\displaystyle\forall\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A},\;\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\in\bigcap_{j=0}^{2}C^{j}([0,t_{\lambda}],H^{2-j}),\;\forall\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A},\;\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\in\bigcap_{j=0}^{1}C^{j}([0,t_{\lambda}],H^{1-j}),

\displaystyle\underline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{evo}_{\lambda}}\in\bigcap_{j=0}^{2}C^{j}([0,t_{\lambda}],H^{2-j}),\;(E^{evo})^{0}\in\bigcap_{j=0}^{2}C^{j}([0,t_{\lambda}],H^{2-j}_{\delta+j}).

Moreover,
Supp(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\underline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{evo}_{\lambda}})\subset B_{S}
.

Proof.

In terms of derivatives, we observe that

\displaystyle\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\sim\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\;\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},

\displaystyle\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\sim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo},

\displaystyle\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\sim\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\;\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}.

More precisely, in the equation for
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
, we use the evolution equation to replace
\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
by its RHS. This produces terms that are at most linear in
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
,
\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
or
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
, and other contributions that belong to
H^{2}
and thus to
L^{\infty}
. We also use the commutator estimates of Section
8.2
. The nonlinearity
\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo})
is handled using the projector on low-frequency, which yields arbitrary regularity gains for any
\kappa>0
. All the remaining nonlinearities are compatible with the regularity stated in the Proposition.
In fact, several of these bounds are derived in detail in the bootstrap Sections
6.3.4
,
6.3.5
, and
6.3.6
. Finally, for the support property, admissibility for KGML ensures

Supp(\textbf{f}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{g}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\underline{\textbf{e}}^{evo},\dot{\underline{\textbf{e}}}^{evo})\subset B_{S^{\prime}}
so that, by finite speed of propagation, we recover the stated support property.
∎

Remark 6.18
.

Combining Propositions
6.10
,
6.15
and
6.17
we obtain

Supp(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}},\underline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}}^{evo},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell})\subset B_{S}
. The quantity
(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}
may not be compactly supported but belongs to
H^{2}_{\delta}
. Nevertheless, every RHS term of the evolution equations (
6.23
) is compactly supported, since
A^{0}
and
(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}
always appear in products with compactly supported terms. Consequently, in the estimates performed in Sections
6.3.4
,
6.3.5
and
6.3.6
, the

||.||_{L^{p}}
norm is effectively taken over
B_{S}
, thus equivalent to
||.||_{L^{p}(B_{S})}
.
Moreover, for a function
f
in
H^{m}_{\delta}
, we also have

||f||_{H^{m}(B_{S})}\leq C_{S}||f||_{H^{m}_{\delta}}.

Hence, we abuse the notation to replace all the weighted norms with classical Sobolev norms in the following estimates.

Remark 6.19
.

When applying Strichartz estimates of Theorem
8.2
or the Lemmas
8.17
and
8.18
to
(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}
, one requires bounds on
||\partial_{t}(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}(0)||_{L^{2}}
and
||(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}(0)||_{\dot{H}^{1}}
. Because
(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}|_{t=0}
(resp.
\partial_{t}(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}|_{t=0}
) is only controlled in
H^{2}_{\delta}
(resp.
H^{1}_{\delta+1}
) for
-3/2<\delta<-1/2
, we have no information on the previous norms. However, as noted in Remark
6.18
, the term
(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}
always appears in products with compactly supported terms. Therefore, the equations only involve
(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}|_{B_{S}}
. The latter coincides with
E^{{}^{\prime}0}_{\lambda}
given by

\begin{cases}\Box E^{{}^{\prime}0}_{\lambda}=\Box(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0},\\
E^{{}^{\prime}0}_{\lambda}(t=0)=(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0}(t=0)|_{\mathscr{I}(T,B_{S})},\\
\partial_{t}E^{{}^{\prime}0}_{\lambda}(t=0)=(\partial_{t}(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{0})(t=0)|_{\mathscr{I}(T,B_{S})},\end{cases}

(6.24)

where
\mathscr{I}(T,B_{S})
denotes the propagation of
B_{S}
defined in Notation
2.4
. Thus, Strichartz estimates can be applied to
E^{{}^{\prime}0}_{\lambda}
, which satisfies the required norm bounds thanks to its compact support.

6.3.3
Assumptions

To extend the time of existence
0<t_{\lambda}
obtained in Proposition
6.17
to the time of existence of the background
T
(for
\lambda
small enough), we use a bootstrap argument. We therefore assume a priori bounds on the coupled evolution components and later show that these bounds can be improved in the following Sections.

Assumptions 6.20
.

There exist
c_{1}>0
and
c_{2}>0
such that for all
\tau\in[0,T]

\begin{split}&\max_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}(\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}(||\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}(\tau)||_{H^{k+1}}+||\partial_{t}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}(\tau)||_{H^{k}})+\lambda||\partial^{2}_{tt}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}(\tau)||_{L^{2}})\leq c_{1}e^{\tau c_{2}},\\
&\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}(||\underline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{evo}_{\lambda}}(\tau)||_{H^{k+1}}+||\partial_{t}\underline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{evo}_{\lambda}}(\tau)||_{H^{k}})+\lambda||\partial^{2}_{tt}\underline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}^{evo}_{\lambda}}(\tau)||_{L^{2}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}c_{1}e^{\tau c_{2}},\\
&\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}(||(E_{\lambda}^{evo})^{0}(\tau)||_{H^{k+1}_{\delta}}+||\partial_{t}(E_{\lambda}^{evo})^{0}(\tau)||_{H^{k}_{\delta+1}})+\lambda||\partial^{2}_{tt}(E_{\lambda}^{evo})^{0}(\tau)||_{L^{2}_{\delta+2}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}c_{1}e^{\tau c_{2}},\\
&\max_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}(\sum_{k\leq 1}(\lambda^{k}||\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(\tau)||_{H^{k}})+\lambda||\partial_{t}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(\tau)||_{L^{2}})\leq c_{1}e^{\tau c_{2}}.\end{split}

(6.25)

Notation 6.21
.

We denote by
C_{0,1,2,\tau}=C_{0,1,2,\tau}(c_{0},\frac{1}{\eta_{0}},N,T,c_{1},c_{2},\tau)
any constant which depends polynomially on
c_{1}e^{\tau c_{2}}
,
\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}}
, and the background constants
c_{0}
,
\frac{1}{\eta_{0}}
,
N=|\mathbb{A}|
and
T
, where
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
are from Assumptions
6.20
. We also recall that
c_{0}
and
T
are provided in Proposition
4.1
,
N
denotes the number of phases introduced in Definition
2.17
, and
\frac{1}{\eta_{0}}
is given by Proposition
2.20
for the compact set
\Omega=B_{S}
from Proposition
4.1
.

We recall that
C_{0}
designates any constant depending only on the background data, see Notation
4.5
. In general, the constants satisfy the hierarchy:

\displaystyle\lambda\ll c_{0}\sim C_{0}<c_{1}\ll c_{2}\ll C_{0,1,2,\tau}.

(6.26)

Only the background constants
c_{0}
and
C_{0}
are fixed. The bootstrap constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
are free parameters at this stage and will be determined later in Lemma
6.37
in order to improve the bootstrap Assumptions
6.20
. Increasing
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
alone is not sufficient to control the nonlinearities. Indeed, nonlinear interactions introduce constants of the form
C_{0,1,2,\tau}
, which grow exponentially with respect to
c_{2}
, in the estimates. In fact, in every occurrence of
C_{0,1,2,\tau}
within the estimates, an additional small factor
\lambda^{\varepsilon}
(for some
\varepsilon>0
) is present. This extra smallness allows the bootstrap bounds to be improved for
\lambda
sufficiently small. We highlight that this mechanism is compatible with the error term being constructed after a first-order approximation.
Now, we give a useful notation before we sum up the discussion on the role of the constants in the bootstrap.

Notation 6.22
.

For
c\in\mathbb{R}
, the notation
\lambda^{c+}
stands for
\lambda^{c+\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon>0
.

To improve the bounds of Assumptions
6.20
, we rely on one or a combination of the following mechanisms, applied term by term:

a)

The estimates depend in fact only on background norms that remain uniformly bounded on
[0,T]
. (
\leq C_{0}
)

b)

The estimates depend on the bootstrap quantities but with a strictly higher power of
\lambda
, so that, for
\lambda
small enough, they can be improved. (
\leq\lambda^{0+}C_{0,1,2,\tau}
)

c)

The estimates depend only linearly on the bootstrap quantities integrated in time. (
C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}}
)

6.3.4
Estimates for
\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}

Throughout this Section, we denote with
C_{0}
any constant that only depends on the background data and with
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
and
C_{0,1,2,\tau}
the constants of the bootstrap. See Notations
4.5
and
6.21
. We also use the schematic Notation
6.1
, where background terms are represented by
\textbf{B}_{0}
and
\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
. The constant
\kappa>0
designates the constant of the projectors
\Pi_{\kappa,-}
and
\Pi_{\kappa,+}
introduced in Definition
2.5
.

The transport equation for
(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}
reads

\forall\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A},\;\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0}.\\

(6.27)

Remark 6.23
.

The error components
(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}
denote schematically
(W^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}},\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}
in the parametrices (
6.10
). The transport equation (
6.27
) represents schematically for every
\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}
,

\begin{cases}\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}W^{+\beta}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=i\partial^{\beta}u_{\mathscr{A}}\overline{\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}\Phi_{0}+\lambda^{-1/2}i\partial^{\beta}u_{\mathscr{A}}\overline{\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}^{evo}+i\partial^{\beta}u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{-}(\overline{\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}^{evo}),\\
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=-i2A^{\alpha}_{0}\partial_{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}-\lambda^{-1/2}i2(E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\Psi_{\mathscr{A}}-i2\partial_{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}((E^{evo}_{\lambda})^{\alpha}\Psi^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}),\\
\end{cases}

(6.28)

where the RHS of the equation for
W^{+\beta}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
is orthogonal to
\partial_{\beta}u_{\mathscr{A}}
.

Remark 6.24
.

The components
(\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}
are introduced specifically to absorb the contribution
\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}
, which corresponds to high-amplitude, high-frequency terms oscillating in characteristic directions and arising from error-background interactions. The transport (
6.27
) is
coupled
with (
6.34
) and (
6.42
).

We now detail the RHS terms and establish the corresponding estimates.

Proposition 6.25
.

The RHS terms

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0},\;\;\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})\;\text{and }\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0}

(6.29)

respectively originate from
\mathscr{L}_{1}\left(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\partial\textbf{B}_{0},\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0},\frac{\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{A}}}{\lambda}\right)
,
\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}\partial\textbf{Z}
and
\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{\mathscr{U}_{\mathbb{A}}}{\lambda}\right)
in (
6.5
).
Under the Assumptions
6.20
, for any
\tau\in[0,T]
and for any
\kappa>0
, the following estimates hold

\displaystyle I:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0}(t)||_{H^{k+1}}dt\leq C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}},

(6.30)

\displaystyle II:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(t)||_{H^{k+1}}dt\leq C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}},

(6.31)

\displaystyle III:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{k+1}}dt\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+}.

(6.32)

Proof.

Estimates for I and II follow from direct applications of Sobolev inequalities of Section
8.3
, the product estimate (
8.29
) of Section
8.6
and the bounds of the Assumptions
6.20
. These terms fall into the case
c)
, since they are linear in the coupled error components. To prove estimate III, we emphasize that the projector
\Pi_{\kappa,-}
is not required to produce additional smallness (see case
b)
). We begin with the
H^{1}
estimate

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{1}}dt

\displaystyle\leq||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],W^{1,\infty})}\int^{\tau}_{0}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{L^{2}}+||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{L^{2}}+||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\nabla\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\underbrace{||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}}_{\text{III.1}}+\underbrace{||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}}_{\text{III.2}}+\underbrace{||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\nabla\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}}_{\text{III.3}}).

For III.1, we use the product inequality (
8.29
) to obtain

\displaystyle III.1\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2}.

Applying Lemma
8.18
, we obtain that, for
\theta\in(0,1/2)
and for
p=2(1-\theta)
,
p^{\prime}=\frac{2(1-\theta)}{1-2\theta}
,

\displaystyle III.2

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{p}([0,\tau],L^{2})}(||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{p^{\prime}}([0,\tau],H^{2})})^{\theta}(||\Box\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(0)||_{\dot{H}^{1}}+||\partial_{t}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(0)||_{L^{2}})^{1-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2}\lambda^{-\theta}(||\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+C_{0})^{1-\theta}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\theta}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+},

\displaystyle III.3

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}||\nabla\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{p}([0,\tau],L^{2})}(||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{p^{\prime}}([0,\tau],H^{2})})^{\theta}(||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{\dot{H}^{1}}+||\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{L^{2}})^{1-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{-\theta/2}(||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+C_{0}\lambda^{1/2})^{1-\theta}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\theta}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+}.

The bound
||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2}
follows from the naive estimates of Proposition
6.35
. Secondly, we treat the
H^{2}
estimate

\displaystyle\lambda\int^{\tau}_{0}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{2}}dt\leq\lambda||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],L^{\infty})}\int^{\tau}_{0}||\nabla\nabla(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\lambda\int^{\tau}_{0}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{W^{1,\infty}}||\nabla(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{L^{2}}+||\nabla\nabla\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{L^{2}}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(t)||_{L^{\infty}}||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{L^{\infty}}+||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{H^{1}}dt}_{\text{III.4}}

\displaystyle\leq III.4+C_{0}\underbrace{\lambda||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\nabla\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}}_{\text{III.5}}+C_{0}\underbrace{\lambda||\nabla\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}}_{\text{III.6}}+C_{0}\underbrace{\lambda||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\nabla\nabla\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}}_{\text{III.7}}.

For III.4, we combine the previous calculations, the Sobolev embeddings of Proposition
8.7
and the product estimate of Proposition
8.16
to
get, for a small
0<\varepsilon<1/2
,

\displaystyle III.4\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\varepsilon}.

For III.6 and III.7, we apply Lemma
8.18
as for III.2 and III.3, losing one
\lambda
in the operation. To estimate III.5, we apply Lemma
8.17
with
1/2>\nu>\varepsilon^{\prime}
and
p=\frac{1}{1-\nu}
, where
\varepsilon^{\prime}
is defined in the naive estimates of Proposition
6.35
, to obtain

\displaystyle III.5

\displaystyle\leq\lambda C_{0}||\nabla\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{p}([0,\tau],H^{1/2-\nu})}(||\Box\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{\dot{H}^{1}}+||\partial_{t}\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+\nu}(||\Box\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+\lambda^{-1/2}C_{0})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+}.

Collecting all contributions, we conclude

\displaystyle III\leq C_{0}(III.1+III.2+III.3+III.4+III.5+III.6+III.7)\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+}.

∎

6.3.5
Estimates for
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}

Throughout this Section, we denote with
C_{0}
any constant that only depends on the background data and with
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
and
C_{0,1,2,\tau}
the constants of the bootstrap. See Notations
4.5
and
6.21
. We also use the schematic Notation
6.1
, where background terms are represented by
\textbf{B}_{0}
and
\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
. The constant
\kappa>0
designates the constant of the projectors
\Pi_{\kappa,-}
and
\Pi_{\kappa,+}
introduced in Definition
2.5
.

The transport equation for
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
reads

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=[(2\partial^{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\partial_{\alpha}+\Box u_{\mathscr{A}}),\Box]\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}+\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}+\Box(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}))

(6.33)

\displaystyle+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})-\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\Box\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\textbf{B}_{0}+[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}.

Remark 6.26
.

The auxiliary component
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
is introduced to control
\Box\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
and compensate for an apparent loss of derivatives as discussed in the introduction about the system (
3.7
). The transport (
6.33
) is obtained by commuting the transport equation for
\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
with the d’Alembertian operator, that is,

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}=[(2\partial^{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\partial_{\alpha}+\Box u_{\mathscr{A}}),\Box]\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}+\Box(\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\mathscr{A}}),

(6.34)

where every occurrence of
\Box\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}
is replaced by
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
. The quantities
\Box\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}
and
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
share identical initial data, see Definition
5.10
, and obey the same transport equation driven by the regular vector field
\textbf{d}u^{\#}_{\mathscr{A}}
. Consequently, they are equal. The equation (
6.33
) is coupled with (
6.27
) and (
6.42
). More explicitly,

\displaystyle[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}=\Box(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0})-\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Box\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0}+\Box(\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0})-\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}.

(6.35)

We analyse the RHS terms and estimate them in the next Proposition.

Proposition 6.27
.

Under the Assumptions
6.20
, there exists
\kappa>0
sufficiently small such that for any
\tau\in[0,T]
, the terms
[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
satisfy

\displaystyle IV:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}},

(6.36)

the commutator satisfy

\displaystyle V:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||[(2\partial^{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\partial_{\alpha}+\Box u_{\mathscr{A}}),\Box]\textbf{F}_{\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}}+C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+},

(6.37)

the remaining terms excluding the projector part satisfy

\displaystyle VI:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\textbf{B}_{0}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}},

(6.38)

\displaystyle VII:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\lambda^{-1/2}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}}+C_{0}+C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+},

(6.39)

and, finally, the terms involving the projector on low frequencies satisfy

\displaystyle VIII:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+},

(6.40)

\displaystyle IX:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\Box(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}))(t)-\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\Box\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+}.

(6.41)

Proof.

The explicit expression of
[\ldots]_{\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}
follows directly from Remark
6.26
.
The estimate for IV is obtained using the bootstrap bounds from Assumption
6.20
and the higher regularity of the background. The corresponding terms are linear in
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
and
\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}
, involve at most one derivative, and therefore fall into case
c)
.

For V, using the commutator estimate (
8.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||[(2\partial^{\alpha}u\partial_{\alpha}+\Box u),\Box]\textbf{F}_{\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}(||\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{H^{k}}+||\partial(\partial^{\alpha}u_{\mathscr{A}}\partial_{\alpha}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+})(t)||_{H^{k}}+||\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}(t)||_{H^{k+1}})dt.

The bounds on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathscr{A}}\partial\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}
provided by Proposition
6.25
, together with the Assumptions
6.20
, yield the desired inequality.

The estimate for VI follows directly from the Assumptions
6.20
and again corresponds to case
c)
. The term VII is controlled using the refined bound on
||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}
obtained in Section
6.3.6
. For both VI and VII, the regularity of the background is exploited. In particular, the fact that one derivative of
\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0}
is in
L^{\infty}
.

We now estimate the low-frequency projector contributions VIII and IX. For
\kappa>0
sufficiently small, the projector, together with the product inequality (
8.29
), provides extra smallness (case
b)
).
We begin with VIII:

\displaystyle VIII

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],W^{1,\infty})}||\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}\lambda^{-\kappa/2}||\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k-1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k-\kappa/2}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],H^{1})}||\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\kappa/2}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+},

where we use the Assumptions
6.20
and the product inequality from Proposition
8.16
for both
k=0
and
k=1
.
Then, for IX, we first develop the term as follows:

\displaystyle IX

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\Box\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}}_{\text{IX.1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\partial\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\partial\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}}_{\text{IX.2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}}_{\text{IX.3}}+\underbrace{\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}}_{\text{IX.4}}.

For IX.1 and IX.2 using the regularity of
u_{\mathscr{A}}
and the smoothing effect of the projector, we obtain, for an arbitrarily small
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle IX.1

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\Box\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],H^{1})}||\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k+1/2+\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k-\kappa-\varepsilon\kappa}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k-1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k-\kappa-\varepsilon\kappa}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{2}([0,\tau],H^{1})}||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{2}([0,\tau],H^{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\kappa-\varepsilon\kappa},

\displaystyle IX.2

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\partial\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],H^{1})}||\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\partial(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}))||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k+1/2+\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k-\kappa-\varepsilon\kappa}||\partial(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k-1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k-\kappa-\varepsilon\kappa}(||\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],H^{k})}||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{1})}+||\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],H^{k})}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{1})})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\kappa-\varepsilon\kappa}.

For IX.3 and IX.4, using the smoothing effect of the projector and the naive estimates
6.35
for
||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}
with
k=0,1
, we obtain

\displaystyle IX.3

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],W^{1,\infty})}||\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k-\kappa}||\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k-1})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k-\kappa}||\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{2}([0,\tau],H^{k})}||\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{L^{2}([0,\tau],H^{1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lambda^{1/2-\kappa},

\displaystyle IX.4

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],W^{1,\infty})}||\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}\lambda^{-\kappa/2}||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k-1/2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sum_{k\leq 1}\lambda^{k}\lambda^{-\kappa/2}||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],H^{1})}||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],H^{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\kappa/2},

which is the desired result for
\kappa<1
. Combining all contributions yields

\displaystyle IX\leq IX.1+IX.2+IX.3+IX.4\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\kappa-\varepsilon}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{0+},

which ends the proof.
∎

Remark 6.28
.

Among the terms appearing in the analysis, the only one that genuinely requires the low-frequency projector is the null form
\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
.

6.3.6
Estimates for
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}

Throughout this Section, we denote with
C_{0}
any constant that only depends on the background data and with
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
and
C_{0,1,2,\tau}
the constants of the bootstrap. See Notations
4.5
and
6.21
. We also use the schematic Notation
6.1
, where background terms are represented by
\textbf{B}_{0}
and
\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}
. The constant
\kappa>0
designates the constant of the projectors
\Pi_{\kappa,-}
and
\Pi_{\kappa,+}
introduced in Definition
2.5
.

The wave equation satisfied by
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
is given by

\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}+\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}(i\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})-\lambda^{1}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})+[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}}.\\

(6.42)

Remark 6.29
.

The component
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
is introduced in order to absorb all remaining terms of the equation (
6.5
). The equation (
6.42
) is coupled with the transport equations (
6.27
) and (
6.33
). More precisely, the term
[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}}
reads

\displaystyle[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{H}\left(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)+(\mathscr{L}_{1}\left(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\partial\textbf{B}_{0},\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0},\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)-\underbrace{\lambda^{-1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}i\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}}_{\text{absorbed by $\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}$}})

\displaystyle+(\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)-\underbrace{\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0}}_{\text{absorbed by $\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}$}}-\underbrace{\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial u_{\mathscr{B}})\textbf{B}_{0}\breve{\textbf{F}}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+}}_{\text{absorbed by $\breve{\textbf{F}}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+}$}})

\displaystyle+(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}-\underbrace{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}}_{\text{explicit in \eqref{eq:schemwaveEevosestiEevo}}}-\underbrace{\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})}_{\text{absorbed by $\breve{\textbf{F}}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+}$}}-\underbrace{\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}}_{\text{explicit in \eqref{eq:schemwaveEevosestiEevo}}}))

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\lambda^{1}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\Box\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}}_{\text{resonant interactions remainder}}-\underbrace{(\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}-\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{B}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\textbf{S}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime}))}_{\text{non-resonant interactions remainder}}+\underbrace{\lambda^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\lambda}}_{\text{background remainder }}.

In the next Propositions, the main estimates for the RHS of (
6.42
) are established.

Proposition 6.30
.

The term
[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}}
is decomposed and estimated term by term. Under the Assumptions
6.20
and for any
\tau\in[0,T]
, the following bounds hold

\displaystyle X:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\mathscr{H}\left(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2+}C_{0,1,2,\tau},

(6.43)

\displaystyle XI:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\left(\mathscr{L}_{1}\left(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\partial\textbf{B}_{0},\textbf{B}^{\prime}_{0},\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)-\lambda^{-1/2}\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}i\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{B}_{0}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\right)(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}},

(6.44)

\displaystyle XII:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\left(\mathscr{L}_{2}\left(\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda},\textbf{B}_{0},\frac{u}{\lambda}\right)-A-B\right)(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}},

(6.45)

\displaystyle\text{for}\;\;A=\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\textbf{F}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}^{+}\textbf{B}_{0}\;\;\text{and}\;\;B=\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}(\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm\partial u_{\mathscr{B}})\textbf{B}_{0}\breve{\textbf{F}}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}^{+},

\displaystyle XIII:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||(\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}\partial\textbf{Z}_{\lambda}-C-D-E)(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2+}C_{0,1,2,\tau}+\lambda^{1/2}C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}},

(6.46)

\displaystyle\text{for}\;\;C=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo},\;\;D=\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,-}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})\;\;\text{and}\;\;E=\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}ie^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}),

\displaystyle XIV:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{C}}\lambda^{1}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\Box\breve{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1}C_{0},

(6.47)

\displaystyle XV:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\left(\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{ell}-\sum_{(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})\in\mathscr{S}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{B}}\pm u_{\mathscr{B}}}{\lambda}}\textbf{S}_{(\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B})}(\textbf{B}_{0}^{\prime})\right)(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0},

(6.48)

\displaystyle XVI:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda||\lambda^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\lambda}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2}C_{0},

(6.49)

where the functions
\mathscr{H}
,
\mathscr{L}_{1}
and
\mathscr{L}_{2}
are given in (
6.5
). Overall, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||[\ldots]_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq C_{0}\lambda^{1/2}+\lambda^{1/2+}C_{0,1,2,\tau}+\lambda^{1/2}C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}}.

(6.50)

Proof.

The quantity X is at least quadratic and involves no derivatives. Hence, the product estimate of Proposition
8.16
yields additional smallness in
\lambda
, corresponding to case
b)
. The terms XI and XII are linear in the coupled error components, which correspond to case
c)
. Their estimates follow directly from Assumptions
6.20
, together with the regularity of the background.

For XIII, since the potentially dangerous contributions are already isolated, the remaining terms are harmless. In particular, the interaction between
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
and
\tilde{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
does not involve any additional power of
\lambda
. However, since
\tilde{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
is treated as a background quantity (i.e., a decoupled error component), the resulting term is effectively linear with respect to
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
(a coupled error component). This corresponds again to case
c)
. The estimate for XIV follows directly from Assumption
6.20
using again the decoupled nature of
\tilde{\textbf{F}}^{+}_{\mathscr{A}\pm\mathscr{B}}
(case
a)
). For XV, we invoke Proposition
6.15
. The bound for XVI follows immediately from Definition
2.28
of
\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\lambda}
and corresponds to case
a)
.

Summing all previous bounds yields (
6.50
).
∎

Proposition 6.31
.

Under the Assumptions
6.20
and for any
\tau\in[0,T]
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle XVII:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}\lambda^{1}\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2+}C_{0,1,2,\tau}.

(6.51)

Proof.

The estimate is obtained by direct computations using the Assumptions
6.20
, since the term is linear in
\textbf{G}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
, corresponding to case
c)
.
∎

Proposition 6.32
.

Under the Assumptions
6.20
, for any
\kappa>0
and for any
\tau\in[0,T]
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle XVIII:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\sum_{\mathscr{A}\in\mathbb{A}}e^{i\frac{u_{\mathscr{A}}}{\lambda}}i\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2+}C_{0,1,2,\tau}.

(6.52)

Proof.

We begin with the
L^{2}
norm:

\displaystyle XVIII.1

\displaystyle:=\int^{\tau}_{0}{||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{L^{2}}dt}

\displaystyle\leq||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],W^{1,\infty})}\int^{\tau}_{0}{||\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{L^{2}}dt}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int^{\tau}_{0}{(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widehat{\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)}|^{2}(\xi)d\xi)^{1/2}dt}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int^{\tau}_{0}{(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\widehat{\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)}|^{2}(\xi)\frac{|\xi|}{|\xi|}d\xi)^{1/2}dt}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lambda^{\kappa/2}\int^{\tau}_{0}{||(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{1/2}}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lambda^{\kappa/2}\int^{\tau}_{0}{||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(t)||_{H^{1}}||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{H^{1}}}dt\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+},

where we use the Plancherel Theorem, the projector on high frequencies
\Pi_{\kappa,+}
, the product estimate of Proposition
8.16
and the Assumptions
6.20
on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
and
\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}
. We observe that the extra factor
\lambda^{\kappa/2}
allows us to recover case
b)
. On the other hand, for the
H^{1}
norm, the projector is not required to get the extra smallness. Using the product estimate of Proposition
8.16
and the interpolation result of Proposition
8.10
, we obtain

\displaystyle XVIII.2

\displaystyle:=\lambda^{1}\int^{\tau}_{0}{||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{1}}dt}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{1}||\partial u_{\mathscr{A}}||_{L^{\infty}([0,\tau],W^{1,\infty})}\int^{\tau}_{0}{||\Pi_{\kappa,+}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{1}}dt}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lambda^{1}\int^{\tau}_{0}{||(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}})(t)||_{H^{1}}dt}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lambda^{1}\int^{\tau}_{0}{||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(t)||_{H^{5/4}}||\textbf{F}^{+}_{\lambda\mathscr{A}}(t)||_{H^{5/4}}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1}\lambda^{1/4}\lambda^{-1/4}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+}.

We conclude that
XVIII\leq XVIII.1+XVIII.2\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+}
, as desired.
∎

It remains to estimate the quadratic term
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
.

Proposition 6.33
.

Under the Assumptions
6.20
and for any
\tau\in[0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle XIX:=\sum_{k\leq 1}\int^{\tau}_{0}\lambda^{k}||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(t)||_{H^{k}}dt\leq\lambda^{1/2+}C_{0,1,2,\tau}.

(6.53)

Remark 6.34
.

Using only product estimates and Sobolev interpolation (Propositions
8.16
and
8.10
), one misses a factor
\lambda^{\varepsilon}
, for
\varepsilon>0
, which prevents closing the bootstrap directly (case
b)
). Indeed, for
0<\alpha<3/2
,
0<\beta\leq 1
and
\alpha+\beta=3/2
we have

\displaystyle||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{2}}\leq||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{H^{\alpha}}||\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{H^{\beta}},

which leads to the cases

\displaystyle 1/2\leq\alpha\leq 1:\;||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(\tau)||_{L^{2}}\leq\lambda^{-1/2+\alpha}(c_{1})^{2}e^{2\tau c_{2}},

\displaystyle 1<\alpha<3/2:\;||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(\tau)||_{L^{2}}\leq\lambda^{1/2}(c_{1})^{2}e^{2\tau c_{2}},

by interpolation. Moreover, for the
\dot{H}^{1}
norm, with the same techniques,

\displaystyle||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(\tau)||_{L^{2}}\leq\lambda^{-1/2}C_{0,1,2,\tau},

\displaystyle||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\nabla\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(\tau)||_{L^{2}}\leq\lambda^{-1/2-\varepsilon}C_{0,1,2,\tau},

where a small
\varepsilon
is lost due to the control of the
L^{\infty}
norm of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}
. The previous estimates yield

\displaystyle XIX\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2-\varepsilon},

at best. In particular, these estimates ignore the time integration and dispersive properties of the wave operator.

Proposition 6.35
.

Let the Assumptions
6.20
hold. For
0<\varepsilon^{\prime}
arbitrarily small, the following naive bounds hold:

\displaystyle||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2}+C_{0}\lambda^{1/2},

\displaystyle||\Box\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{-1/2-\varepsilon^{\prime}}+C_{0}\lambda^{-1/2}.

(6.54)

Proof.

The result follows by combining Propositions
6.30
,
6.31
, and
6.32
, together with the estimates of Remark
6.34
. The factor
C_{0}\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{\tau c_{2}}
is absorbed into
C_{0,1,2,\tau}
.
∎

We now have enough material to prove Proposition
6.33
.

Proof of Proposition
6.33
.

Define

\displaystyle XIX.1:=||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})},

\displaystyle XIX.2:=\lambda||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\nabla\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})},

\displaystyle\;\;\;XIX.3:=\lambda||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}.

Applying Lemma
8.18
, with
\theta\in(0,1/2)
and exponents
p=2(1-\theta)
and
p^{\prime}=\frac{2(1-\theta)}{1-2\theta}
, we obtain

\displaystyle XIX.2

\displaystyle\leq\lambda||\nabla\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{p}([0,\tau],L^{2})}(||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{p^{\prime}}([0,\tau],H^{2})})^{\theta}(||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+||\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{\dot{H}^{1}}+||\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{L^{2}})^{(1-\theta)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2}\lambda^{-\theta/2}(||\Box\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+c_{1}\lambda^{1/2})^{(1-\theta)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+},

where the last inequality follows from the naive estimates of Proposition
6.35
.

The same argument directly apply to
XIX.1
to obtain

\displaystyle XIX.1\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+}.

To estimate
XIX.3
, we apply Lemma
8.17
with
1/2>\nu>\varepsilon^{\prime}
and
p=\frac{1}{1-\nu}
, where
\varepsilon^{\prime}
is defined in Proposition
6.35
, to get

\displaystyle XIX.3

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lambda||\partial\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{p}([0,\tau],H^{1/2-\nu})}(||\Box\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+||\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{\dot{H}^{1}}+||\partial_{t}\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}(0)||_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{1+\nu}(||\Box\nabla\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\lambda}^{evo}||_{L^{1}([0,\tau],L^{2})}+c_{1}\lambda^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+}.

Combining all contributions yields

\displaystyle XIX\leq C_{0}XIX.1+XIX.2+XIX.3\leq C_{0,1,2,\tau}\lambda^{1/2+}.

This ends the proof of Proposition
6.33
.
∎
